DUFM AMJJA SOND J FMAMJ JASOND J FMAMJJASOND J FMAMJJASONDJ FMAMJ JASOND JEM A 2023 Source: CFRA, S&P Global Market Intelligence Past performance is not an indication of future performance and should not be relied upon as such. Analysis prepared by Angelo Zino, CFA on Dec 07, 2023 04:32 PM ET, when the stock traded at USD 128.37. Highlights \\U2022 We see sales rebounding 21% in 2024 and 10% in 2025 after our view of a 4.2% decline in 2023 (+44% in 2022). Client/PC sales rose 42% in Q3 but were partly offset by declines in Gaming (- 8%) and Embedded (-5%). Data Center was flat. We are encouraged by the PC recovery, believe customer inventories have been sharply curtailed, and are excited about content gains from Al servers. We remain extremely optimistic about AMD's data center pipeline on both the CPU and GPU side, with its 3 nm Turin offering [based on Zen 5) to ramp in 2024 while data center GPU revenue (Instinct MI250/MI300 GPUs] is now seen reaching $400M in Q4/$2B in 2024 (implies 7%-8% of 2024 sales). \\U2022 We estimate margins to expand to 54% in 2024 and to 55% in 2025 on more stable PC volume and comparable to our 51% gross margin forecast for 2023. Over time, we see higher volumes on new product launches (EPYC, Radeon, and Ryzen processors) supporting a better mix. Greater representation from AMD's higher margin Data Center and Embedded segments will also aid margins over time. \\U2022 We view AMD's balance sheet as healthy ($3.3B net cash) and see sharply improving free cash flow (FCF) ahead (+$3.5B forecast in 2023 and rising to +$6B in 2025). Investment Rationale/Risk \\U2022 Our Buy rating reflects our growth outlook for AMD's CPU data center servers from the ramp of its next-generation EPYC processor, prospects for GPU offerings (Instinct MI250/ MI300 GPUs to ramp in Q4 and 2024], and our expectation for balance sheet improvement. Margins will expand in the 2H and for 2024, off cyclical trough, on better mix from new product launches as well as PC-related stabilization/ recovery. We positively view Al customer engagements, specifically from cloud provider Microsoft, as we see AMD as a suitable #2 supplier in the data center GPU space (NVIDIA #1). We believe AMD's Al opportunities stem much further than the cloud market given its wide variety of offerings (e.g., CPUs, GPU, FPGAs, and ASICs). \\U2022 Risks to our recommendation and target price include lower demand for computers and servers, greater-than-anticipated pricing pressure, significant market share loss, and slower-than-expected global economic growth. \\U2022 Our 12-month target price of $150 is based on a P/E of 27.6x our 2025 EPS estimate, above peers. Improving Al prospects, margin expansion potential, attractive net cash/FCF, and greater earnings leverage ($5-$6 per share in 2025] should drive multiple expansion. Business Summary Nov 01, 2023 CORPORATE OVERVIEW. Advanced Micro Devices (AMD] designs and sells digital integrated circuits (ICs), including x86 microprocessors and chipsets for computers, embedded microprocessors for commercial and consumer applications, and as a result of the company's acquisition of ATI Technologies in October 2006, graphics processors. Beginning with the second quarter of 2022, AMD changed its segment reporting to the ollowing four segments: Data Center, Client, Gaming, and Embedded The Data Center segment primarily includes server microprocessors, GPUs, data processing units (DPUs), Field Programmable Gate Arrays (FPGAs), and adaptive System-on-Chip (SoC) products for data centers. The Client segment primarily includes microprocessors, accelerated processing units (APUs) that integrate microprocessors and graphics, and chipsets for desktop and notebook personal computers. The Gaming segment primarily includes discrete graphics processing units (GPUs), semi-custom SoC products, and development services. The Embedded segment primarily includes embedded microprocessors, GPUS, FPGAS, adaptive SoC products, and Adaptive Compute Acceleration Platform (ACAP) products. A microprocessor is an IC that serves as the central processing unit (CPU), or brain, of a computer. The performance of a processor is a critical factor for the performance of the computer. The main measures for microprocessor performance include: work-per-cvcle, or how many instructions per cvcle, clock speed, how fast the CPU's internal logic operates as measured by units of hertz, and power consumption. Other factors impacting performance include the number of cores on a microprocessor, bit rating of the microprocessor, memory size, and data access speed. A GPU is a programmable logic chip that helps render images, animations, and video and is increasingly being used to handle general computing tasks. GPUs are located in plug-in cards, as a discrete processor or in a chip on the motherboard, or in the same chip as the CPU as part of an APU or SoC. GPUs on stand-alone cards or discrete GPUs on the motherboard typically access their own memory, while GPUs in the chipset or CPU chip share main memory with the CPU. A server is a computer system that performs services for connected customers as part of a client-server architecture. Many servers are designed to run an application or applications, often for extended periods of time with minimal human intervention. Examples of servers include cloud, web, e-mail, print, and on- premise servers. Servers are also used in cloud computing, which is a computing model where data, applications, and services are delivered over the internet or an intranet, which can be rapidly provisioned and released with minimal effort. Embedded products address computing needs in enterprise-class telecommunications, networking, security, and storage svstems. Typically, AMD embedded products are used in applications that require high to moderate levels of performance, where key features may include relatively low power, small form factor, and 24x7 operations. AMD also leverages its core IP, including its graphics and processing technologies, to develop semi-custom solutions for the PC and gaming markets. In this market, semiconductor suppliers work alongside system designers and manufacturers to enhance the performance and overall user experience for semi-custom customers. AMD has used this type of collaborative co-development approach with many of today's leading game console manufacturers and can also address customer needs in many other markets beyond game consoles. CORPORATE STRATEGY. The microprocessor market is a critical part of the semiconductor industry, and is dominated by two companies, Intel and AMD. The two competitors have battled for preeminence in the segment for decades. Intel now ships over 80% of the world's microprocessors, and is still the clear leader in this space. It has accomplished this by extending its leadership in kev technoloaies that have provided important competitive advantages. Meanwhile, AMD accounts for less than 20% of the microprocessor market and has made many changes over the years to build a sustainable business model. MPACT OF MAJOR DEVELOPMENTS. In February 2022, AMD acquired XLNX in an all-stock transaction valued at over $50 billion. XLNX shareholders received 1.7234 shares of AMD stock per XLNX share, which equated about 26% ownership in the combined company for XLNX investors. We view the transaction positivel om the perspective that brings together complementary product offerings (CPUs and GPUs from AMD FPGAs from XLNX). We note that XLNX also increases AMD's addressable market, with opportunities from the emergence of edge computing (e.g., automotive). The deal was immediately accretive. FINANCIAL TRENDS. AMD's revenues are highly cyclical and tend to move with the broader semiconductor market. We note that there are industry-specific factors, such as average selling prices and sales mix, that have also caused variability in annual sales results. The swings in sales have caused wide changes in margins because of the company's fixed cost structure that included plants and equipment, and when sales dropped with the broader economy during the last recession, losses ensued. However, we see a steadier margin profile following the spin-off of its manufacturing arm in 2009. Revenue of $23.6 billion grew 44% in 2022 driven by higher Embedded, Data Center, and Gaming segment revenue, partially offset by lower Client segment revenue. Pro forma revenue rose 20% on a combined AMD and Xilinx company basis. Non-GAAP gross margin widened to 52% from 48% in 2022, on a richer product mix with hiaher Embedded and Data Center revenue, partially offset by lower Client revenue As of the end of September 2023, AMD's debt obligations were $2.5 billion, while cash was $5.8 billion. We anticipate sharply higher free cash flow generation ahead, estimating more than $5 billion in 2024, which compares to the $3.1 billion posted in 2022. Despite near-term uncertainty, we see improving results across AMD's business, specifically related to planned new product releases, supporting a stronger balance sheet in the coming years. CFRA Sub-Industry Outlook We have a positive fundamental outlook for the semiconductors sub-industry over the next 12 months. We expect semiconductor sales to rebound about 12% in 2024 after our forecast for a 12% decline in 2023, with a cyclical recovery that began in the second half of last year [Y/Y revenue growth returned in Q4 2023). We do think the rebound in 2024, from a component perspective, will be driven by memory, while the industry also benefits from higher capital spending on greater GenAl capabilities (e.g., new computing and networking tools] among the major cloud providers. Investors should be mindful that the chip industry is undergoing cycles within cycles, with data centers gaining momentum and more mature/cyclical PC/smartphone orders having officially bottomed out. Analog chips tied to industrial/auto markets are still in decline, but content growth and undershipping true demand will set up that space for an order rebound as we progress into the second half of 2024. Memory is poised to witness the most notable rebound, as we favor the fundamentals on the DRAM side, given Al prospects are tied to high bandwidth memory (HBM]. Although inventories remain above historical levels, we believe industry capacity cuts have significantly improved the supply/demand landscape, with further improvement expected through 2024. For the most part, investments from key players will be focused on technology upgrades rather than capacity upgrades until industry profitability significantly rebounds (we view calendar year 2025 as more of an investment year). We would get skeptical of the trajectory of the recovery if macro conditions deteriorate and/or major competitor Samsung were to all of a sudden boost capex spend. We look for the accelerator space to grow at least 70% to 80% in 2024. Ever since Microsoft's monumental decision to boost investments in OpenAl, our forecast for the accelerator space has continued to go up. The major beneficiary has, of course, been GPUs, which have been designated as the key to greater machine learning/inferencing capabilities in the cloud, but we do see a place for both FPGAs and ASICs looking ahead. On the GPU side, we expect supply constraints to begin to improve, while AMD's ramp with its MI300X GPU will also help support demand for accelerators and NVDA will launch next-gen GPUs. Chipmakers tied to the data center are exposed to the best financially positioned customers on the planet, with prospects tied to Al extremely encouraging. We believe the semiconductor industry will witness a super cycle over the next decade as content growth per device across a host of markets accelerates, driven by the emergence of greater Al capabilities. Given this, we estimate the semiconductors industry will grow at a 10-year annualized pace of 6%-7%, or at least 2x global GDP. While companies that outsource manufacturing would typically have more stability in gross margins (a trade-off for capped upside), others with their own manufacturing see more variability. Industry margins are a function of manufacturing utilization and inventory supply-demand imbalances. We forecast some companies, especially in analog, to maintain higher inventory levels. Healthy free cash flow generation and high cash levels will likely result in greater shareholder return via share repurchases. Regulatory and geopolitical uncertainties remain notable risks. For full-year 2023, the S&P semiconductors sub-industry index rose 104.1% vs. a 23.4% increase for the S&P 1500 index. / Angelo Zino, CFA Industry Performance GICS Sector: Information Technology Sub-Industry: Semiconductors Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Jan 27, 2024 NasdaqGS:AMD - Sub-Industry S&P 1500 720% 640% 560% 480% - 400% \\U2022 320% 240% 160% 80% - 0% 2019 2020 2021 2022 2023 2024 NOTE: A sector chart appears when the sub-industry does not have sufficient historical index data. Analyst Research Notes and other Company News December 07, 2023 03:26 PM ET... CFRA Maintains Buy Opinion on Shares of Advanced Micro Devices, Inc. [AMD 128.39****): At an Al-focused event yesterday, AMD's Lisa Su and team were upbeat about prospects for the broader accelerator space as well as planned GPU (MI300) ramp. Although AMD restated that it sees $2B worth of Al chip demand in 2024, it has supply for above that level, which we think implies higher revisions to planned forecasts given the current demand for GPUs. AMD also announced partnerships with Meta, OpenAl, and Microsoft that will support orders. On a broader level, Lisa Su cited that revenue for Al accelerators will grow to at least $400B by 2027 (+70% CAGR], drawing excitement as her prior forecast was $150B. Although we expect NVIDIA to remain the dominant share provider of GPUs, AMD offers a great second alternative and we think it's poised to take at least a 5%-10% share over time that will help drive significant upside to revenue over the next three years. We keep estimates unchanged, but boost our 12-month target to $150 from $125 (27.6x our 2025 EPS view] to reflect higher growth/EPS expectations. / Angelo Zino, CFA November 01, 2023 12:19 AM ET... CFRA Maintains Buy Opinion on Shares of Advanced Micro Devices, Inc. (AMD 87.57****): According to an unconfirmed Bloomberg report, Microsoft (MSFT *****) is working with AMD on the expansion into Al processors. It appears as if Microsoft is providing financial support to AMD to develop a custom processor chip that Microsoft will use for Al workloads, code-named Athena. Despite limited details, we view it as an overall positive for AMD as it bolsters the thesis that the company will be among a host of winners within the semiconductor industry, as we move towards a more Al- enabled world. That said, we would highlight that this deal is unlikely to have an impact on AMD's financial results over the next 12 months, but could further solidify the relationship the two parties have across other offerings. Also, we would not view the news as a negative for peer NVIDIA (NVDA ****). In fact, we view it as a positive for NVDA as it likely indicates that demand for GPUs and other Al-enabled chips (e.g., ASICs) is very strong, and to some extent, supply-constrained given the needs of cloud providers. / Angelo Zino, CFA May 03, 2023 08:03 AM ET... Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B C B+ Average Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such CFRA 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security will command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, anc orivate market valuation metrics, including Fair Value. Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ** * * * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling in price on an absolute basis. Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Disclosures Stocks are ranked in accordance with the following ranking methodologies: STARS Stock Reports: Qualitative STARS rankings are determined and assigned by equity analysts. For reports containing STARS rankings refer to the Glossary section of the report for detailed methodology and the definition of STARS rankings. Quantitative Stock Reports: Quantitative rankings are determined by ranking a universe of common stocks based on 5 measures or model categories: Valuation, Quality, Growth, Street Sentiment, and Price Momentum. In the U.S., a sixth sub-category for Financial Health will also be displayed. Percentile scores are used to compare each company to all other companies in the same universe for each model category. The five (six) model category scores are then weighted and rolled up into a single percentile ranking for that company. For reports containing quantitative rankings refer to the Glossary section seof the report for detailed methodology and the definition of Quantitative rankings. STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports: The methodologies used in STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports (collectively, the \\\"Research Reports\\\") reflect different criteria, assumptions and analytical methods and may have differing rankings. The methodologies and data used to generate the different types of Research Reports are believed by the author and distributor reasonable and appropriate. Generally, CFRA does not generate reports with different ranking methodologies for the same issuer. However, in the event that different methodologies or data are used on the analysis of an issuer, the methodologies may lead to different views on the issuer, which may at times result in contradicting assessments of an issuer. CFRA reserves the right to alter, replace or vary models, methodologies or assumptions from time to time and without notice to clients. Unless otherwise indicated, there is no intention to update this document. 1\\n\\nStock Report | March 29, 2025 | NasdaqGSSymbol: AMZN | AMZN is in the S&P 500 Amazon.com, Inc. Business Summary Mar 10, 2025 corporate Uverview. Since opening for business as \\\"Earth's Biggest Bookstore\\\" in July 1995 and going public 1 1997 at a split-adjusted stock price of $1.50 per share, Amazon.com has expanded into a number of other product categories including apparel, shoes, and jewelry; electronics and computers; movies, music, and games; toys, kids, and baby; sports and outdoors; home and garden; tools, auto, and industrial; grocery; health and beauty; and digital downloads. Over the years, Amazon has expanded into several service offerings, including fulfillment, advertising, publishing, subscriptions (e.g., Amazon Prime membership), as well as its leading cloud computing business called Amazon Web Services (AWS). AWS offers a broad set of on-demand technology services, including compute, storage, database, analytics, and machine learning. Amazon now makes more money selling services compared to products. Services made up 57% of total revenues in 2024, up from 55% in 2023, 53% in 2022, 49% in 2021, and 44% in 2020. In Amazon's 2021 shareholder letter (released in April 2022), the company stated it had over 200 million Prime customers worldwide. We think Amazon could be approaching 300 million Prime customers, driven by international expansion. Amazon has not provided Prime customer figures since the 2021 shareholder letter. The breakdown of 2024 net sales by reporting segments is as follows: North America (61%); International (22%); and Amazon Web Services, or AWS (17%). We note AWS has grown from 11% of total revenue mix in 2018 to 17% of total revenue mix in 2024. The international segment returned to profit in 2024 (operating income of $3.8 billion) following a loss between 2021 through 2023. AMZN is still in the early stages of its e-commerce business in many emerging markets (AMZN has launched in 10 countries over the last seven years; it took about a decade to become profitable in the U.S.]. North America saw $25.0 billion in operating income in 2023, up from $14.9 billion in 2023 and an operating loss of $2.9 billion in 2022. AWS saw $39.8 billion in operating profit in 2024, up from $24.6 billion in 2023 and $22.8 billion in 2022. The breakdown of 2024 revenues by channels is as follows: online stores, 37%; physical stores, 3%; third- party seller services, 24%; subscriptions services, 7%; advertising services, 9%; Amazon Web Services, 18%; Primary Business Dynamics. Amazon's business is highly intensive in terms of operating and capital expenditures. Net capital investments totaled $78 billion in 2024, up from $48 billion in 2023, driven by a surge in technology and infrastructure investments within AWS. Total net capital investments are expected to be north of $100 billion in 2025, as AMZN continues to invest in capacity to meet Al/cloud demand. E- commerce is a less capital intensive business versus AWS, but we nonetheless expect a step up in investments related to automation, robotics, and same-day facilities. Over the last few years, Amazon has been laser focused on lowering its cost to serve, including finding efficiencies in fulfillment and transportation. MGA. In February 2023, Amazon acquired One Medical, a primary care provider, for about $3.9 billion. In March 2022, Amazon closed on its deal to acquire MGM Holdings Inc. (MGM) for $8.45 billion, its second largest acquisition ever. Amazon's largest acquisition was Whole Foods (a leading retailer of natural and organic foods) in August 2017 for $13.7 billion in cash. In June 2020, it acquired Zoox, a start-up provider of autonomous vehicles, for about $1.2 billion. In September 2018, the company acquired PillPack, an online pharmacy company, for about $753 million. In February 2018, Amazon acquired Ring, a start-up provider of smart home technology applications, for about $839 million. Key Developments. In September 2023, the FTC filed a much-anticipated lawsuit against Amazon, alleging that the company is a monopolist using anticompetitive and unfair strategies to stifle competition, penalize third-party sellers, and maintain high prices for consumers. Amazon disputes any wrongdoing and intends Amazon has partnered with Rivian Automotive, an electric vehicle manufacturer, to add electric delivery vehicles. Amazon olans to have 100.000 electric delivery vehicles from Rivian on the road by 2030. As of December 31, 2024, Amazon held 158 million shares of Rivian's Class A common stock, representing about 14% ownership interest and 13% voting interest. The fair value of Amazon's investment in Rivian was $2.1 billion as of 2024 vs. $3.7 billion as of 2023, $2.9 billion as of 2022, and $15.6 billion as of 2021. AMZN shares were added to the Dow Jones Industrial Average (DJIA) on February 26, 2024. Financial Trends. Revenues grew 11% in 2024 vs. 12% in 2023, 9% in 2022, 22% in 2021, 38% in 2020, and 21% in 2019. On a five-year compounded annual growth rate (between 2019 and 2024], revenue grew about 18%, led by advertising (+34%), followed by AWS (+25%), third-party seller services (+24%), subscription services (+18%), and first-party online stores (+12%). Operating income was $68.6 billion as of 2024 (10.7% margin), up from $36.9 billion as of 2023 (6.4% margin], and $12.2 billion as of 2022 (2.4% margin). North America margins were 6.4% in 2024 vs. 4.2% in 2023, and -0.9% in 2022. International margins were +2.7% in 2024 vs. -2.0% in 2023, and -6.6% in 2022. AWS margins were 37.0% in 2024 vs. 27.1% in 2023, and 28.5% in 2022 Amazon had about $101 billion in cash and marketable securities as of 2024, up from $87 billion in 2023 and $70 billion in 2022. The company did not repurchase any common shares in 2024 or 2023, following about $6.0 billion of share repurchases in 2022. As of December 31, 2024, Amazon had $6.1 billion remaining under its repurchase program. Amazon has never declared or paid cash dividends. CFRA Sub-Industry Outlook CFRA has a positive 12-month fundamental outlook for the Broadline Retail sub-industry. This sub-industry includes general and discount merchandise retailers, department stores, and e- commerce retailers and marketplaces, selling mostly discretionary consumer merchandise. We expect consumer spending to remain resilient over the next year, driven by a solid labor market, positive real wages, the wealth effect from a strong housing and stock market, and easing inflation. Our view is supported by recent data points indicating the strength of the U.S. consumer. For example, retail sales (ex-auto/gas) grew fairly consistently throughout 2024, including better-than-expected growth during the 2024 holiday season. In addition, the U.S. added 2.2 million jobs in 2024, the third consecutive month of strong job creation. Stronger hiring and low layoffs helped lift average hourly earnings, which grew about 4% in 2024. We see e-commerce retailers driving growth in the sub-industry. E-commerce sales, which saw a massive boost at the onset of the pandemic, continue to outpace brick-and-mortar sales as retailers invest in omnichannel shopping. Based on retail sales data from the U.S. Census Bureau, nonstore retail sales, which primarily include sales from e-commerce companies, grew 9.8% Y/Y in November 2024, the strongest growth since March 2024 (+11.0% Y/Y). Based on the Quarterly Retail E-commerce Sales Report from the U.S. Census Bureau, e-commerce sales in Q3 2024 accounted for 16.2% of total sales, up from 15.4% in the year-ago quarter and 11.2% at the end of 2019 (just prior to the pandemic). We see department stores and certain discount merchandise retailers as underperformers within this sub-industry. Due to ongoing secular headwinds and signs of overexpansion, many mall-based retailers are closing stores. For example, Macy's plans to close about 150 underperforming stores over the next few years (66 store closures expected in 2025). Many mall- based retailers have already filed for bankruptcy due to overleveraged balance sheets (e.g., Industry Performance JCPenney, Neiman Marcus). We see big-box retailers like Walmart, Target, and Costco taking market share from department stores over the next several years. We see big-box retailers also gaining share in pharmacy, driven by the closures of drug retailers like CVS, Walgreens, and Rite Aid. Amazon.com (AMZN] is by far the largest constituent within the sub-industry with over 90% weighting. While AMZN is a major e- commerce player, the company also has a very large cloud computing business, along with many ancillary businesses. In 2024, the Broadline Retail sub-industry advanced 43.4% compared to the 26.7% increase in the Consumer Discretionary sector and the 22.2% advance for the SSP 1500. We note the Broadline Retail sub-industry is a relatively new GICS classification (created in March 2023). General Merchandise Stores and Department Stores were merged into Broadline Retail, which also includes certain retailers from the now discontinued Internet & Direct Marketing Retail sub-industry (e.g., AMZN, EBAY, and ETSY). / Arun Sundaram, CFA, CPA GICS Sector: Consumer Discretionary Sub-Industry: Broadline Retail Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Mar 29, 2025 - NasdaqGS:AMZN - Sub-Industry S&P 1500 160% - 120% - 80% - 40% + 0% -40% - 2021 2022 2023 2024 2025 NOTE: A sector chart appears when the sub-industry does not have sufficient historical index data. All Sector & Sub-Industry information is based on the Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS). CFRA Lifts Opinion on Shares of Amazon.com, Inc. to Strong Buy from Buy [AMZN 199.25*****): We keep our $276 12-month target, using a 18x EV/EBITDA multiple on our 2025 adj-EBITDA estimate of $169.0B (+17% Y/Y, slightly up from $168.9B] vs. the 12x- 30x historical range. We cut our 2025 adj-EPS to $8.32 from $8.34 and 2026's to $9.63 from $9.67. We see the recent pullback as an enhanced buying opportunity, with AMZN shares trading at a 13x forward EV/EBITDA multiple vs. the 20x long-term mean. Notably, AMZN only traded below 10x during the 2008 financial crisis. Despite recent macro concerns (tariffs, recession risks), we believe AMZN is well positioned to navigate through near-term volatility. Tariff risks are manageable, in our view, given the company's diverse supplier network and its ability to lean more into its third-party/fulfillment business. While AMZN is not recession proof, we do think it is more recession resilient than peers given its diversified business model, dominant positions in e-commerce, cloud computing, and streaming, and relatively affluent Prime membership base. / Arun Sundaram, CFA, CPA February 27, 2025 05:01 PM ET... CFRA Keeps Buy Opinion on Shares of Amazon.com, Inc. [AMZN 208.74****): Yesterday, Amazon introduced Alexat, a new, more intelligent and conversational personal Al assistant, built using models from Amazon Bedrock (e.g., Amazon Nova, Anthropic Claude). The service will soon be available in the U.S. on Alexa-enabled devices, along with a new mobile app and web-based browser. Prime members will receive it for free, while non-Prime members will be charged $19.99 monthly. We'd expect AMZN to better monetize this service over time given the high cost of running Al models at this scale. With over 600 million Alexa devices sold globally (probably at least 100 million active devices out there), Alexa could become a significant revenue source and profit center for AMZN. That said, AMZN is playing catch-up to other more popular GenAl-powered assistants, and users today mostly use Alexa for menial tasks like timers and playing music. Overall, we're pleased to finally see an upgrade to Alexa following many upgrade rumors and reports of how unprofitable the devices business currently is. / Arun Sundaram, CFA, CPA February 06, 2025 09:16 PM ET... Though AWS margins will likely be down in 2025 from increased reinvestment, total company margins should still be up due to e-commerce efficiencies and faster growth of higher-margin businesses like AWS and ads. / Arun Sundaram, CFA, CPA February 06, 2025 05:14 PM ET... Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance. Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B C B+ Average Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such CFRA 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security wil command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, and private market valuation metrics. Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ** * * * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling in price on an absolute basis. Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Disclosures Stocks are ranked in accordance with the following ranking methodologies: STARS Stock Reports: Qualitative STARS rankings are determined and assigned by equity analysts. For reports containing STARS rankings refer to the Glossary section of the report for detailed methodology and the definition of STARS rankings. Quantitative Stock Reports: Quantitative rankings are determined by ranking a universe of common stocks based on 5 measures or model categories: Valuation, Quality, Growth, Street Sentiment, and Price Momentum. In the U.S., a sixth sub-category for Financial Health will also be displayed. Percentile scores are used to compare each company to all other companies in the same universe for each model category. The five (six) model category scores are then weighted and rolled up into a single percentile ranking for that company. For reports containing quantitative rankings refer to the Glossary section seof the report for detailed methodology and the definition of Quantitative rankings. STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports: The methodologies used in STARS Stock Reports and Quantitative Stock Reports (collectively, the \\\"Research Reports\\\") reflect different criteria, assumptions and analytical methods and may have differing rankings. The methodologies and data used to generate the different types of Research Reports are believed by the author and distributor reasonable and appropriate. Generally, CFRA does not generate reports with different ranking methodologies for the same issuer. However, in the event that different methodologies or data are used on the analysis of an issuer, the methodologies may lead to different views on the issuer, which may at times result in contradicting assessments of an issuer. CFRA reserves the right to alter, replace or vary models, methodologies or assumptions from time to time and without notice to clients. Analyst's Risk Assessment LOW MEDIUM HIGH Our risk assessment reflects the historical cyclicality of the semiconductor equipment industry, a lack of visibility in the intermediate term, the dynamic nature of changes in semiconductor technology, and intense competition. This is offset by AMAT's market leadership, size, and its solid balance sheet. Business Summary May 20, 2022 CORPORATE OVERVIEW. Applied Materials (AMAT) is the worldwide leader in the manufacturing of semiconductor capital equipment. AMAT divides its business into three segments: Semiconductor Systems Group, Applied Global Services, and Display and Adjacent. The Semiconductor Systems Group, which accounted for 70% of FY 21 sales, is focused on developing and selling equipment for use in the front end of the semiconductor fabrication process. This segment develops, manufactures, and sells a range of manufacturing equipment used to fabricate semiconductor chips. This segment includes equipment used for various steps of the chip making process, including the transfer of patterns into device structures, transistor and interconnect fabrication, metrology, inspection and review, and packaging technologies for connecting finished IC die. This segment's patterning systems and technologies address challenges resulting from shrinking pattern dimensions and the complexity in vertical stacking found in semiconductor devices. Its transistor and interconnect products and technologies enable continued power and performance improvements of 3D transistors. AMAT's metrology, inspection and review systems' imaging capabilities and algorithms employ optical and e-beam technologies to meet technical demands in areas, including self-aligned double and quad patterning, extreme ultraviolet layers, measurement-intensive optimal proximity correction mask qualification, and new 3D architectures. Its packaging technologies address challenges resulting from the increasing heterogeneous integration of multiple IC dies in a single package. The company delivers capabilities that enable chipmakers to establish statistical process control, ramp up production runs, and achieve high production yields. The company's equipment is sold to integrated device manufacturers and foundries worldwide. The Applied Global Services segment, which represented 22% of FY 21 sales, provides solutions to optimize and increase productivity at customers' fabs (semiconductor fabrication facilities). The segment includes products and services to improve the efficiency and reduce operating costs at semiconductor, display, and solar customer factories. Applied Global Services products consist of spares, services, certain earlier generation products, and remanufactured equipment. Display and Adjacent markets, which accounted for 7% of FY 21 sales, develops equipment for the fabrication of flat panel displays. The Display and Adjacent Markets segment is comprised of products for manufacturing liquid crystal displays (LCDs), organic light-emitting diodes (OLEDs), and other display technologies for TVs, monitors, laptops, personal computers (PCs), electronic tablets, smartphones, and other consumer-oriented devices as well as equipment for processing flexible substrates. While similarities exist between the technologies utilized in semiconductor and display fabrication, the most significant differences are in the size and composition of the substrate. Substrates used to manufacture display panels, and other devices are typically glass, although newer flexible materials are entering the market. Display and Adjacent Markets industry growth depends primarily on consumer demand for increasingly larger and more advanced TVs and high resolution displays for mobile devices as well as new form factors, including thin, light, curved and flexible displays, and new applications such as augmented and virtual reality. CORPORATE STRATEGY. AMAT intends to use its economies of scale and worldwide distribution and support capability to fend off competition and grow market share. We think AMAT has been successful in leveraging its size and what we see as its broad expertise across segments, with limited exceptions in certain niche segments. We think AMAT benefits from a strong track record of quality products and reliable service, making it difficult for competitors to take market share. In our view, AMAT will continue to pursue acquisitions that will allow it to leverage its knowledge and expertise into new areas. MARKET PROFILE. The semiconductor industry is driven by demand for advanced electronic products, including smartphones and other mobile devices, servers, personal computers, automotive electronics, storage, and other products. Applied offers a variety of differentiated products that must continuously evolve to satisfy customers' requirements to compete effectively in the marketplace. There are a number of competitors serving the semiconductor manufacturing equipment industry, with some offering a single product line and others offering multiple product lines. FINANCIAL TRENDS. AMAT's business has historically been highly cyclical. In periods of strong growth in demand for semiconductors, the chipmakers optimistically extrapolate growth, leading to over-investment in equipment, which subsequently leads to a period of reduced equipment purchases. We think AMAT is less cyclical than many of its peers due to its size and product diversification. Redistribution or reproduction is prohibited without prior written permission. CFRA Sub-Industry Outlook Industry Performance Our fundamental outlook for the semiconductor equipment sub-industry for the next 12 months is positive. We believe foundry customers are sustaining increasingly elevated levels of investment, while memory customers face larger headwinds (softening demand & lower ASPs) and are more likely to cut capex. We see chip equipment sales growing by 15% in '22 after rising approximately 25% in '21. Political drivers have also emerged as a potential catalyst. With China controlling an increasing amount of the supply chain, the U.S. and Europe are both looking to boost their own chip manufacturing presence, which we believe will be done via smaller and less efficient fabs, boosting Wafer Fabrication Equipment (WFE) capital intensity and benefiting equipment suppliers. We estimate that memory spending will likely account for about 40% to 50% of total revenue in '22 and believe memory spending in 2023 falls to the lower end of this range. We believe memory market is likely to roll over in average selling prices for both DRAM & NAND in 2H '22. We believe previous consolidation in the memory space will allow customers to better sustain a healthy long- term industry landscape and create more predictable cyclical corrections moving forward. We also believe that slight capex cuts from memory firms will ultimately provide a more balanced & healthy semiconductor market, despite near term headwinds for equipment. In addition, we think China's desire to grow its local semiconductor manufacturing industry is supporting the demand for equipment. Foundry spending through '22 will likely be driven by 7-nm & below investments, as customers add capacity on these advanced technology nodes. We estimate spending by foundry manufacturers will likely grow by more than 20+% in '22, driven by aggressive spending initiatives from industry leaders Taiwan Semiconductor ($40B - $44B in '22 vs $25B - $28B in '21) as well as Intel's upgraded capital expenditures guidance ($25B- $28B in '22 vs. $18B-$19B in '21), and Samsung planning to triple its foundry capacity by '26. Foundries' share in total capital expenditures should be about 45% to 50% over the next year, based on our estimates. Semiconductor equipment sales are highly concentrated, with Intel, Taiwan Semiconductor, and Samsung expected to comprise ~60% of total sales in the coming year. We also note that equipment providers exposed to the flat panel display industry should also benefit from greater penetration for Organic LED (OLED) displays in long-term, offset by softening consumer demand in 2H '22. Long term, we see rising orders supported by the emergence of new products as well as improving demand for devices such as computers and handsets. We think customers are placing orders on advanced machinery at lower technology nodes, and we believe the release of lower-priced/more efficient mobile devices should provide additional growth. For the most part, we believe balance sheets from the largest equipment providers are healthy and could sustain pronounced cyclical downturns similar to those seen in the past. For 21 and 22, we expect free cash flow to increase, which will support greater share repurchases and higher dividend payments to maximize shareholder value. YTD through April 16, 2022, the S&P Semiconductor Equipment Index declined 33.8%, versus a drawdown of 20.5% for the S&P 1500. For the twelve months through 2021, the SSP Semiconductor Equipment Index rose 52.3% versus an increase of 26.7% for the SSP 1500. / Keven Young GICS Sector: Information Technology Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Equipment Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Aug 13, 2022 NasdaqGS:AMAT - Sub-Industry. CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Recommendation on Shares of Applied Materials (AMAT 93.25*****): We cut our 12-month target to $145 from $177 on a P/E of 16.7x our CY 23 EPS view of $8.65, near large semi-equip peers. We are encouraged by certain transitory input costs abating and applied deploying certain price adjustments to offset higher costs in coming quarters. Long-term secular drivers for semi-equip remain in place, numerous legislative catalysts incentivizing countries to localize chip manufacturing and the building of recurring revenue streams for chip equipment manufacturers in form of services contracts due to the value add to customers from intelligent equipment. We expect AMAT to continue to return 80-100% of FCF to shareholders in form of buybacks & dividends, forecasting $5.8B in FY 22 & $7B in FY 23. / Keven Young 06:55 AM ET... Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance. Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B C B+ Average Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such CFRA 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security will command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, anc orivate market valuation metrics, including Fair Value. Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively. Quantitative Stock Reports: Quantitative rankings are determined by ranking a universe of common stocks based on 5 measures or model categories: Valuation, Quality, Growth, Street Sentiment, and Price Momentum. In the U.S., a sixth sub-category for Financial Health will also be displayed. Percentile scores are used to compare each company to all other companies in the same universe for each model category. The five (six) model category scores are then weighted and rolled up into a single percentile ranking for that company. Analysis prepared by Jun Zhang Tan, CFA on Jul 21, 2022 02:42 AM ET, when the stock traded at USD 514.00. Highlights Investment Rationale/Risk \\U2022 ASML's 02 2022 net sales of EUR5.4 billion were slightly ahead of its guidance, but we find it impressive that its bookings were strong at record quarterly of EUR8.5 billion, including EUR5.4 billion of EUV and High NA systems. Despite some weakness in certain end markets, the overall demand for ASML's system remains strong driven by node migration. ASML is still expecting to ship 55 EUV systems in 2022, but it will only recognize 40 instead of previous target of 49 due to the effect of fast shipments resulting in revenue deferral. 2022 net sales growth guidance is reduced from 20% to 10% due to EUR2.8 billion delay in revenue recognition, but this also implies upsides for 2023 net sales. \\U2022 Q2 2022 EBIT of EUR1.6 billion (+33% Y/Y) was slightly ahead of consensus estimates. However, ASML's Q3 2022 implied EBIT guidance of EUR1.5 billion (-20% Y/Y) is 37% below consensus estimates. Due to the revenue deferral related to high margin EUV systems and cost inflation, ASML expects 2022 gross margin to be 49-50% vs. previous guidance of 52%. This implies high teens cut in consensus EBIT estimates. \\U2022 We adjust our 2022 and 2023 EPS to EUR14.50 (EUR17.51) and EUR21.50 (EUR20.50) to reflect the guidance. \\U2022 ASML is in the monopoly position in supplying EUV tools to both logic and memory clients. As ASML's clients are expanding the capacity to meet the demands and migrating to more advanced nodes, this should continue to drive ASML's sales in the medium term. The cost inflation and revenue deferral headwinds will drag 2022 gross margin to 49-50% level, but we believe that the focus should be on the strong bookings for its advanced EUV systems. A positive revenue mix will lead to a sustainable gross margin. More important, ASML is studying feasibility to increase its 2025 capacity production for low and high NA EUV to 90 and 20 units, above its previous CMD targets and consensus expectations, suggesting strong medium-term outlook remains intact. \\U2022 Downside risks include: (i) extent of EUV adoption being less than expected, (ii) slower economic growth, which impacts semiconductor demand, and (iii) geopolitical tension. \\U2022 We lower our target price for ASML to USD755 (from USD892), representing a 35x 2022 EV/ EBITDA. The valuation is a premium to its five- year historical average of 24x, justified in our view by its monopoly position and the accelerating adoption of its EUV systems. Analyst's Risk Assessment LOW MEDIUM HIGH Our risk assessment reflects the historical cyclicality of the semiconductor equipment industry, the lack of visibility in the medium term, the dynamic nature of changes in semiconductor technology, and intense competition. These risks are only partly offset by the company's strong market position, size, and financial condition. Business Summary Jan 24, 2022 COMPANY BACKGROUND: Headquartered in Veldhoven, ASML Holding N.V. was created initially as a joint venture between Philips and Advanced Semiconductor Materials International in 1984. Today, ASML is a world-leading semiconductor equipment company with more than 14,000 employees and counting all of the top chipmakers as its customers. It is primarily involved in the design, manufacturing, and marketing and servicing of photolithography systems. The company's shares are listed on both Euronext Amsterdam and NASDAQ, while major shareholders include Capital Group International (15.28%) and BlackRock (7.69%) as of PRODUCTS OVERVIEW: The company's key products - photolithography systems - are essential to the chips manufacturing process in which light is used to project circuit patterns onto silicon wafers coated with light-sensitive materials, thereby engraving or enabling the formation of the exposed patterns on the wafers. To create more powerful chips, an increasing amount of transistors has to be crammed onto the wafers, and it is imperative that the size of the transistors has to be shrunk. This is limited by the wavelength of the light source utilized in the systems. The development of Extreme Ultraviolet (EUV) lithography technology is ASML's answer to the challenge of sub-10 nm manufacturing whereby a EUV light source with a wavelength of only 13.5 nm is utilized. ASML sees a healthy semiconductor end-market growth fueled by major innovation drivers, such as 5G connectivity, artificial intelligence, autonomous driving, and big data. This translates into growth of worldwide capacity in all segments, especially at the leading-edge nodes, creating opportunities for ASML that is near-monopoly in advanced lithography MARKETS OVERVIEW: ASML serves two main groups of customers with different end-market - Memory and Logic. The first customer group is involved in the manufacturing of memory chips, which are used to store data and could be further divided into NAND-Flash and DRAM, whereby the former could retain data without power and is increasingly used to replace magnetic storage media (primarily hard disks), while the latter primarily serve to enhance the performance of electronic products. Logic chips (or microprocessors), on the other hand, function as information processors in electronic devices. Manufacturers that both design and manufacture microprocessors are referred to as Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDM], while those specialized only in the manufacturing process are known as foundries. In 2021, ASMLs net bookings increased to EUR26.24 billion (EUR11.29 billion), driven by both memory and logic customers. In addition to the structural demand for EUV systems, ASML also benefitted from capacity constraints faced by the global semiconductor industry. COMPETITIVE LANDSCAPE: ASML has a clear technology lead over peers, in our view, which should allow the company to gain market share in the semiconductor equipment sector and insulate it to some degree from industry cyclicality. The company has more than 80% share of the global lithography equipment market and is dominant in the high-end segments, while major competitors - Canon and Nikon - lead in the low-end segments such as i-line and ArF technology. In view of the significant capital investments required to develop new lithography technology, we believe ASML will remain ahead of its competitors with its EUV development, which received backings from major customers, including Intel, Samsung, and TSMC through a co-investment program. FINANCIAL TRENDS: ASML operates in a highly cyclical industry supplying a small number of customers and hence its financial performance is volatile. During the period 2017-2021, ASML's revenue has grown at a CAGR of 20%, driven by growth in net system sales and net services and field option sales. The gross margin has increased from 44.9% to 52.7% during the same period, thanks to product mix improvement. We expect the margin to improve further as the contribution of higher-ASP EUV systems increase in the future. We note that the proportion of group revenues from services has increased, driven by the growing installed base of lithography machines. This will help to stabilize its earnings going forward as we believe service revenues are far more resilient to down cvcles in the semiconductor industry. With ASML's roadmap to ship next- generation EUV being on track, we think this should help in ASML's pricing and gross margin. ASML's EBIT has grown at an impressive CAGR of 29% during 2017-2021 and we see room for margin expansion going forward. In its recent CMD, ASML is targeting EUR24-30 billion revenue and gross margin of 54-56% in 2025, which we believe are achievable. Sub-Industry Outlook Our fundamental outlook for the semiconductor equipment sub-industry for the next 12 months is positive. We believe foundry customers are sustaining increasingly elevated levels of investment, while memory customers face larger headwinds (softening demand & lower ASPs) and are more likely to cut capex. We see chip equipment sales growing by 15% in '22 after rising approximately 25% in '21. Political drivers have also emerged as a potential catalyst. With China controlling an increasing amount of the supply chain, the U.S. and Europe are both looking to boost their own chip manufacturing presence, which we believe will be done via smaller and less efficient fabs, boosting Wafer Fabrication Equipment (WFE) capital intensity and benefiting equipment suppliers. We estimate that memory spending will likely account for about 40% to 50% of total revenue in '22 and believe memory spending in 2023 falls to the lower end of this range. We believe memory market is likely to roll over in average selling prices for both DRAM & NAND in 2H '22. We believe previous consolidation in the memory space will allow customers to better sustain a healthy long- term industry landscape and create more predictable cyclical corrections moving forward. We also believe that slight capex cuts from memory firms will ultimately provide a more balanced & healthy semiconductor market, despite near term headwinds for equipment. In addition, we think China's desire to grow its local semiconductor manufacturing industry is supporting the demand for equipment. Foundry spending through '22 will likely be driven by 7-nm & below investments, as customers add capacity on these advanced technology nodes. We estimate spending by foundry manufacturers will likely grow by more than 20+% in '22, driven by aggressive spending initiatives from industry leaders Taiwan Semiconductor ($40B - $44B in '22 vs $25B - $28B in '21) as well as Intel's upgraded capital expenditures guidance ($25B- $28B in '22 vs. $18B-$19B in '21), and Samsung planning to triple its foundry capacity by '26. Foundries' share in total capital expenditures should be about 45% to 50% over the next year, based on our estimates. Semiconductor equipment sales are highly concentrated, with Intel, Taiwan Semiconductor, and Samsung expected to comprise ~60% of total sales in the coming year. We also note that equipment providers exposed to the flat panel display industry should also benefit from greater penetration for Organic LED (OLED) displays in long-term, offset by softening consumer demand in 2H '22. Long term, we see rising orders supported by the emergence of new products as well as improving demand for devices such as computers and handsets. We think customers are placing orders on advanced machinery at lower technology nodes, and we believe the release of lower-priced/more efficient mobile devices should provide additional growth. For the most part, we believe balance sheets from the largest equipment providers are healthy and could sustain pronounced cyclical downturns similar to those seen in the past. For 21 and 22, we expect free cash flow to increase, which will support greater share repurchases and higher dividend payments to maximize shareholder value. YTD through April 16, 2022, the S&P Semiconductor Equipment Index declined 33.8%, versus a drawdown of 20.5% for the S&P 1500. For the twelve months through 2021, the SSP Semiconductor Equipment Index rose 52.3% versus an increase of 26.7% for the SSP 1500. / Keven Young Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Equipment Peer Group*: Semiconductor Equipment Recent Stock Price 561.30 22.11 108.27 66.73 294.00 105.47 382.02 503.15 112.55 317.22 100.93 CFRA Industry Performance GICS Sector: Information Technology Sub-Industry: Semiconductor Equipment Based on SSP 1500 Indexes Five-Year market price performance through Aug 20, 2022 NasdaqGS:ASML - Sub-Industry S&P 1500 480% 400% 320% * 240% 160% + 80% - 2018 2019 2020 2021 2022 NOTE: A sector chart appears when the sub-industry does not have sufficient historical index data. CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Opinion on ADSs of ASML Holding [ASML 514.00*****): We lower our target price for ASML to USD755 (from USD892), representing a 35x 2022 EV/ EBITDA. 02 2022 net sales and EBIT were slightly above consensus estimates. However, the bookings were surprisingly strong at EUR8.46 billion, of which EUR5.4 billion was for EUVs including High NA. Despite some slowdown in consumer-related products, ASML has not seen any demand reduction for its systems. Therefore, ASML is still expecting shipment of 55 EUV systems for 2022. However, ASML will only recognize 40 systems in 2022 vs. previously 49 systems due to fast shipments, which will result in deferred revenue. At the same time, EBIT margin will be affected by inflation. ASML's Q3 2022 EBIT implied guidance is 37% below consensus estimates, and ASML cuts 2022 revenue growth rate to 10% from 20% due to the impact of deferred revenue. CFRA Maintains Buy Opinion on ADSs of ASML Holding (ASML 859.00****]: We raise our target price to USD967 [from USD826), representing a 35x (30x) 2022 EV/EBITDA. The valuation is a premium to its 5-year historical average of 24x, justified in our view by its monopoly position and the accelerating adoption of its EUV systems. Ahead of ASML's 2021 Investor Day on September 29, 2021 and Q3 2021 results release on October 20, 2021, ASML's share price performance has done well for most part of 2021. In its previous Investor Day in 2018, ASML targeted EUR15-24 billion sales by 2025. However, ASML will likely raise the medium-term guidance as the consensus estimates are EUR28.5 billion for 2025. Despite the high expectations, ASML has consistently beat consensus estimates. We believe it will likely continue to do so, supported by its monopolistic position in EUV, ongoing node migration, structural growth drivers such as 5G and cloud computing, and product mix improvement. We keep our higher-than-consensus forecasts unchanged, reflecting our positive view on ASML. / Jun Zhang Tan, CFA April 21, 2022 11:08 PM ET... CFRA Maintains Strong Buy Opinion on ADSs of ASML Holding (ASML 620.00*****): We maintain our target price at USD892, representing a 35x 2022 EV/EBITDA. Q1 2022 EBIT was 5% above consensus estimates thanks to better-than-expected sales and lower R&D expenses. Q2 2022 sales guidance is EUR5.1-EUR5.3 billion vs. consensus' EUR5.5 billion, but this is due to EUR800 million revenue deferral into Q3 2022. The underlying demand remains very strong, supported by exceptionally strong net bookings of EUR7 billion and an order book of EUR29 billion. Thus, ASML is confident it can deliver 20% sales growth for 2022, which we believe is achievable given the long lead time of system delivery is less susceptible to the short-term semiconductor downcycle risk. ASML only expects 1%-pt adverse impact to its 53% gross margin target for 2023 due to cost inflation, which we find commendable in a supply constrained environment. More important, ASML is studying feasibility to increase its 2025 capacity production above current consensus estimates, suggesting a strong medium-term outlook remains intact. / Jun Zhang Tan, CFA 03:33 AM ET... Glossary STARS Since January 1, 1987, CFRA Equity and Fund Research Services, and its predecessor S&P Capital IQ Equity Research has ranked a universe of U.S common stocks, ADRs (American Depositary Receipts), and ADSs (American Depositary Shares) based on a given equity's potential for future performance. Similarly, we have ranked Asian and European equities since June 30, 2002 Under proprietary STARS (Stock Appreciation Ranking System), equity analysts rank equities according to their individual forecast of an equity's future total return potential versus the expected total return of a relevant benchmark (e.g., a regional index (MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, MSCI AC Europe Index or S&P 500\\U00ae Index]], based on a 12-month time horizon. STARS was designed to help investors looking to put their investment decisions in perspective. Data used to assist in determining the STARS ranking may be the result of the analyst's own models as well as internal proprietary models resulting from dynamic data inputs. S&P Global Market Intelligence's Quality Ranking (also known as S&P Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings) - Growth and SSP Capital IQ Earnings & Dividend Rankings stability of earnings and dividends are deemed key elements in establishing S&P Global Market Intelligence's earnings and dividend rankings for common stocks, which are designed to capsulize the nature of this record in a single symbol. It should be noted, however, that the process also takes into consideration certain adjustments and modifications deemed desirable in establishing such rankings. The final score for each stock is measured against a scoring matrix determined by analysis of the scores of a large and representative sample of stocks. The range of scores in the array of this sample has been aligned with the following ladder of rankings: A+ Highest High A Above B C B+ Average Below Average B- Lower Lowest D In Reorganization NC Not Ranked EPS Estimates CFRA's earnings per share (EPS] estimates reflect analyst projections of future EPS from continuing operations, and generally exclude various items that are viewed as special, non-recurring, or extraordinary. Also, EPS estimates reflect either forecasts of equity analysts; or, the consensus (average) EPS estimate, which are independently compiled by S&P Global Market Intelligence, a data provider to CFRA. Among the items typically excluded from EPS estimates are asset sale gains; impairment, restructuring or merger-related charges; legal and insurance settlements; in process research and development expenses; gains or losses on the extinguishment of debt; the cumulative effect of accounting changes; and earnings related to operations that have been classified by the company as discontinued. The inclusion of some items, such 12-Month Target Price The equity analyst's projection of the market price a given security will command 12 months hence, based on a combination of intrinsic, relative, anc orivate market valuation metrics, including Fair Value. CFRA Abbreviations Used in Equity Research Reports CAGR - Compound Annual Growth Rate CAPEX - Capital Expenditures CY - Calendar Year DCF - Discounted Cash Flow DDM - Dividend Discount Model EBIT - Earnings Before Interest and Taxes EBITDA - Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation & Amortization EPS - Earnings Per Share EV - Enterprise Value FCF - Free Cash Flow FFO - Funds From Operations FY - Fiscal Year P/E - Price/Earnings P/NAV - Price to Net Asset Value PEG Ratio - P/E-to-Growth Ratio PV - Present Value R&D - Research & Development ROCE - Return on Capital Employed ROE Return on Equity ROl - Return on Investment ROIC - Return on Invested Capital ROA - Return on Assets SG&A - Selling, General & Administrative Expenses SOTP - Sum-of-The-Parts WACC - Weighted Average Cost of Capital Dividends on American Depository Receipts (ADRs) and American Depository Shares (ADSs) are net of taxes (paid in the country of origin). Reflects an equity analyst's view of a given company's operational risk, or the risk of a firm's ability to continue as an ongoing concern. The Qualitative Risk Assessment is a relative ranking to the U.S. STARS universe, and should be reflective of risk factors related to a company's operations, as opposed to risk and volatility measures associated with share prices. For an ETF this reflects on a capitalization-weighted basis, the average qualitative risk assessment assigned to holdings of the fund. STARS Ranking system and definition: **** * 5-STARS (Strong Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark, by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares rising in price on an absolute basis. **** * 4-STARS (Buy): Total return is expected to outperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ** * * * 3-STARS (Hold): Total return is expected to closely approximate the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. ***** 2-STARS (Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark over the coming 12 months. **** * 1-STAR (Strong Sell): Total return is expected to underperform the total return of a relevant benchmark by a notable margin over the coming 12 months, with shares falling in price on an absolute basis. Relevant benchmarks: In North America, the relevant benchmark is the S&P 500 Index, in Europe and in Asia, the relevant benchmarks are the MSCI AC Europe Index and the MSCI AC Asia Pacific Index, respectively.
